# FFA / OEV — Current Status (updated 20 Aug 2026)

One line per active or recently-closed track. Full detail is below, newest generally near the top but not guaranteed — check dates. Closed self-contained sagas (Clip Extractor, Flatcam, Runner failsafe) live in `docs/ai-project-state-archive.md`.

- **YouTube cookies -> R2 for ffa-media worker — NEW, feature branch, BLOCKED on secrets.** `sync-yt-cookies.yml` (branch `feature/sync-yt-cookies-r2`, `5bf0f544`) reuses the existing live-Chrome-profile sync from `clip-extractor.yml` unchanged, exports it via `yt-dlp --cookies-from-browser` to Netscape format, and uploads to R2 at `secrets/yt_cookies.txt` on a 4-hourly schedule independent of Clip Extractor's 6-hourly one. Not merged, not dispatched. **Needs 4 new repo secrets before it can run past the upload step: `S3_ENDPOINT_URL`, `S3_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `S3_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `S3_BUCKET`** (same values already set on ffa-media's Railway services — none of the 4 currently exist as Actions secrets in this repo, confirmed via API). `ffa-media` side (`feature/worker-r2-cookie-sync`) is ready to consume the object once it exists. See `ffa-media/docs/ai-project-state.md` for the consumer side.
- **OEV zero-copy CUDA/Vulkan interop — RESOLVED, in production.** Shared-VkBuffer architecture merged (`video-stitcher@b2fc622...`), rolled out to standard FFA workflows. Direct shared-`VkImage` approach is a dead end — do not reopen without genuinely new evidence.
- **Frame-stride 3 — OPEN REGRESSION after multi-sample visual validation (16 Aug).** The full-rate sparse-analysis implementation is live in code, but it is **not product-accepted as the default**: `sample_01` 180s looked mostly watchable with slight misses, while `sample_02` 180s showed severe lag/near-static camera behaviour for large portions. Treat stride 1 v4 as the accepted quality reference until the cadence/timebase issue is resolved.
- **Ball-state recovery / panner containment — ACTIVE, major progress 17 Aug.** Backward bridging now keeps `world.ball` populated on ~98.6% of `sample_02` stride-1 frames despite raw YOLO missing ~45.4%; the remaining dominant failure is camera containment when a known ball is not given enough authority. A direct ball-containment override is currently in a test-only A/B and is **not yet product-validated**.
- **YOLO26m vs YOLOv8n — YOLO26m preferred for current follow-cam experiments.** Prior A/B evidence showed roughly half the track-loss rate (~38 vs ~80 over 180s), higher confidence (~0.907 vs ~0.819), for ~7% more processing time. Keep visual product acceptance separate from detector metrics.
- **RunPod test allocator — HARDENED and proven; capacity/host compatibility remains variable.** Experiment path now excludes a GPU type after real NVDEC failure and falls through the widened pool; RTX 5090 is excluded from this experiment after a real Reco detector smoke failed with `cudaErrorNoKernelImageForDevice`. Capacity remains the main transient failure mode.
- **Network volume map — OPEN maintenance debt.** EU-RO-1 current; EUR-IS-1/US-IL-1 are stale caches; AP-JP-1's volume no longer exists on RunPod. `OEV_NETWORK_VOLUME_MAP` update blocked on a GitHub token permissions error (403).
- **OEV/Reco licensing (AGPL-3.0) — OPEN, commercial blocker before customer-facing launch.** Full review needed before OEV is sold; not a technical blocker for current testing. See "Frame-stride testing + production rollout — FINAL HANDOFF" below for detail.
- **VLM-assisted ball recovery — NEXT ARCHITECTURE TICKET, not implemented yet.** Keep YOLO/tracker/bridge as the cheap primary path; invoke a contextual reasoner only for genuine unresolved/ambiguous ball state. The VLM must return a candidate hypothesis (`A/B/C/none`), never control the camera directly or mutate trusted state without validation. Design the interface so a commercial API backend can later be replaced by an OEV-owned specialist model.
- **P010 10-bit real-fixture test — not yet done.** Compiled/unit-tested only.
- **Standardized 180s sample validation — ACTIVE.** `sample_01` through `sample_05` have been rendered on the current stride-3 experiment path; `sample_02` exposed a severe visual regression. A same-sample stride-1 v4 control (`31939820386`) completed successfully and is ready for visual A/B. Internal processing-time variance across samples is the next measurement task.

## Ball-state bridging, panner containment, and VLM recovery direction — ACTIVE (17 Aug 2026)

**Current product-quality finding:** the project has now separated **ball-state knowledge** from **camera framing**. Raw YOLO26m ball recall on `sample_02` remains poor (~54.6% present / ~45.4% missing), but the merged backward bridge reconstructs most of those gaps. On the clean stride-1 bridging run `31980187523`, `world.ball=None` fell to **150 / 10,775 frames = 1.39%**, with **4,714 Bridged frames** and longest remaining `None` gap **71 frames (~1.18s at 60fps)**. This is the strongest evidence so far that detector misses and camera misses are now separate problems.

**Backward bridge status — MERGED / proven on the test sample:** `JhnsonO/video-stitcher` main includes the genuine-anchor backward bridge at `c8b0d74b537d192c7de8d2856de64620a82830cf`. `TrackState::Bridged` interpolates between genuine Tracking anchors found inside lookahead; Bridged states cannot recursively anchor more bridge states. The bridge is deliberately not a detector replacement: long gaps with no plausible future anchor can still remain unknown.

**Responsive panner tuning run — completed, useful but insufficient:** run `31997785854` completed successfully on `sample_02`, 180s, stride 1, YOLO26m, lookahead 1.5s with the agreed small-pitch tuning (`ball_weight=0.70`, `dead_zone_rad=0.06`, `cluster_alpha=0.08`, `velocity_alpha=0.08`, `max_velocity_rad_per_sec=0.31`, dynamic FOV 38/44/58 degrees, no v4 damping). Event reanalysis still found the tracked ball outside the horizontal rendered frustum on **17.71% of known-ball frames**; combined tracker-derived ball non-containment was ~**18.86%**. Treat this as a horizontal telemetry approximation, not a full 2D visibility metric. Several multi-second misses occurred while YOLO and the tracker were actively Tracking the ball, proving that more detector recall alone cannot solve the remaining camera failure.

**Why the panner can ignore a known ball:** the current `FieldPanner` starts from the densest player cluster, gives the ball only a weighted pull while it is considered close enough to that cluster, and its lookahead target prefers future player-cluster context whenever a cluster exists. Therefore `world.ball` can be correct while the resolved camera target remains far away. Product priority is now explicit: **ball containment first, action/player context second, smoothness third, aesthetic composition fourth.**

**Direct containment experiment — IN PROGRESS, not validated / not merged:** test branch `test/oev-ball-containment-override-01`, branch head `5394502f4a77a99e4e26225f4b800db3c0279a52`, workflow run `32000972073`. Production `video-stitcher/main` remains unchanged. The test-only runtime patch adds an opt-in containment state for trusted `Tracking`/`Bridged` balls: enter when ball offset reaches **0.80 x current half-FOV**, leave only after it returns inside **0.45 x half-FOV** (hysteresis); during containment the target is the ball directly, player-cluster composition/cluster lookahead and the normal dead-zone do not get to hold the camera elsewhere, and FOV can widen toward the configured 58-degree ceiling. Do **not** report this as successful until the run completes and the video + telemetry are reviewed. If it works, promote the behavior through normal Reco code review rather than keeping a runtime patch.

**Next major ball-recovery architecture — VLM/context fallback, NOT IMPLEMENTED YET:** stop treating every YOLO miss as a VLM call. The intended hierarchy is `YOLO/detector -> candidate generation -> tracker/coast/backward bridge -> uncertainty trigger -> contextual reasoner -> validation gate -> trusted WorldState -> panner`. Candidate generation must remain separate from trusted ball-state mutation (same architectural correction already required for tiled/high-res reacquisition).

**VLM v1 contract:** invoke only when deterministic tracking/bridge remains genuinely uncertain or several plausible candidates compete. Exploit OEV's offline nature by supplying selected frames **before and after** the uncertain interval, previous trusted ball state, next trusted ball state when available, low-confidence/high-resolution ball candidates, full-frame/panorama context, and player positions/movement already available from Reco. Prefer a constrained answer such as **candidate A / B / C / none + confidence** rather than asking a general model to invent arbitrary ball coordinates.

**Validation gate:** a VLM answer is a **hypothesis only**. Before mutating `WorldState`, validate trajectory/velocity plausibility, consistency with later trusted observations, field bounds, stationary/spare-ball rejection, candidate motion and any other deterministic evidence available. Log every invocation, candidate set, context window, backend response, confidence, accept/reject reason and resulting state transition. The VLM must never directly control the camera.

**Backend abstraction / cost path:** introduce a provider-neutral `BallRecoveryReasoner`-style interface. An API VLM can be the first experimental backend; if credentials/provider wiring is undesirable, implement a deterministic mock/replay backend first so the recovery pipeline is testable without network calls. Long term, the intended commercial cost path is to train/fine-tune a compact OEV-specific temporal/candidate-ranking model on accumulated hard recovery cases and reserve a larger VLM for rare escalations — do **not** attempt to train a general foundation VLM from scratch.

**Commercial/IP design constraint:** keep the implementation independently derived and document architectural decisions. Do not use a competitor patent as an implementation specification or deliberately recreate a disclosed competitor pipeline. A formal freedom-to-operate review is still required before commercial launch; this is separate from the current technical prototype work.

**Immediate next actions:** (1) finish and visually/telemetrically evaluate containment run `32000972073`; (2) if containment works, convert it from test-only patch to reviewed Reco behavior and re-run the same sample; (3) start the VLM fallback ticket with the provider-neutral reasoner interface, uncertainty trigger, candidate package and validation gate; (4) keep the first VLM experiment isolated from panner tuning so ball-recovery quality can be measured independently.

## Frame-stride 3 multi-sample validation — REGRESSION FOUND; stride-1 control rendered (16 Aug 2026)

**Current verdict:** stride 3 is **not accepted for product-quality follow-cam yet**, despite the earlier 30s speed benchmark and `sample_01` 180s looking reasonably watchable. Multi-sample testing exposed a severe camera-response regression on `sample_02`. The accepted quality reference remains the v4 stride-1 behaviour from run `31913398625` (YOLO26m, FOV 44°, lookahead 1.5s, `cluster_alpha=0.08`, v3 trajectory hysteresis + v4 micro-damping).

**Implementation that is being tested:** Reco `b2fc622f4b07dfa0c43e3ad9a96ac85b4f450085` keeps full-rate source rendering while running detector/tracker/panner only on analysis frames (`source_index % frame_stride == 0`), reuses `session.last_world_state.clone()` on render-only frames, and interpolates camera poses between sparse anchors. FFA main rollout commit `a729c5281ef1cb50046ce4ad721bdb1352ebf91e` enabled `--frame-stride 3` on the normal testing scripts. The earlier same-sample 30s benchmark (~2.737x faster stitch wall time) remains valid as a speed result; it is **not sufficient quality evidence**.

**Adapter/allocator fixes while getting the real 180s test to execute:** experiment branch `experiment/lookahead-ball-containment-01`. Bootstrap nested-quote bug fixed at `5c3d9309c8c605b090e3da0a8c17db311cf0c090`; the YOLO26 stride wrapper anchor was moved to the unique final base-execution boundary and validated before paid dispatch (fix lineage includes `21b3774`). These were harness defects, not Reco quality findings. The broadened allocator (`d3167ba9ca135c4a7bb53c24f77db703cec572f0`) proved GPU-type failover. RTX 5090 is excluded from this experiment because run `31918562049` passed Vulkan/CUDA/NVDEC/basic ORT but the real Reco detector smoke failed `cudaErrorNoKernelImageForDevice` on the YOLO QuickGelu kernel. Run `31918951122` then showed the remaining dominant failure mode: after rejecting a bad 4090, the compatible fallback types were out of capacity.

**Standardized 180s sample batch:** sample set `GX010197-seed1384188843`, same v4 camera settings + YOLO26m + lookahead 1.5 + alpha 0.08, with stride 3 as the intended cadence change. `sample_01` completed after capacity retries and Johnson judged it to have only a few slight misses and to be broadly watchable. The remaining batch all completed workflow-successfully from experiment SHA `f75abe28eb98b85ae749ee11d52b4aec2f68a75c`: `sample_02` run `31938283243`, `sample_03` `31938286396`, `sample_04` `31938289343`, `sample_05` `31938292331`. **Green workflow status is execution evidence only; visual quality remains the acceptance gate.**

**Decisive visual finding:** Johnson reviewed `sample_02` and reported that the camera struggled to keep up with the ball for large portions and that even at the start the camera barely moved. This is materially worse than the small misses in `sample_01` and is enough to reject the present stride-3 tuning pending the A/B control.

**Leading hypothesis — plausible, not yet proven by the control:** the accepted v4 limits/hysteresis are expressed per analysis update rather than in real time. Stride 3 drops meaningful panner updates from ~60Hz to ~20Hz but leaves values such as max yaw `0.75°/update`, confirmation `18` updates, and missing-ball hold `24` updates unchanged. In real-time terms that can turn roughly `45°/s` camera authority into `15°/s`, `18` updates from ~0.3s to ~0.9s, and `24` from ~0.4s to ~1.2s. That matches the observed slow-response failure shape, but do **not** call it proven until the same-sample stride-1 output is visually checked. The robust eventual fix should make limits/hysteresis time-aware/source-frame-aware rather than blindly multiplying every constant by 3.

**Clean same-sample control:** branch `experiment/sample02-v4-stride1-control`, head `6bc0857208b3d82a4f764a4f9658e42fdfc2057e`, run `31939820386`. It keeps the current b2fc Reco, broadened allocator, v4 stabilizer adaptation, YOLO26m, lookahead 1.5 and alpha 0.08, but does **not** inject `--frame-stride 3`. Workflow completed SUCCESS. **Visual comparison against stride-3 `sample_02` is still pending**; do not infer the product verdict from CI success alone.

**Processing-time variance — active investigation, do not conflate with pod setup:** for the four 180s batch runs, the outer `Upload + run sample-baseline test script` step lasted `6m17s` (`sample_02`), `18m26s` (`sample_03`), `13m05s` (`sample_04`), and `13m04s` (`sample_05`). This excludes allocator/bootstrap but is still only an outer step duration, **not yet the authoritative internal Reco processing time**. Next task is to pull each run's internal Reco/render timing, GPU model and FPS, normalize by the identical 180s source duration, and separate decode / YOLO / tracking+panner / stitch+render / encode where telemetry permits. Root-cause the ~3x spread; do not hand-wave it as different scenes being harder without log evidence.

**Immediate next actions:** (1) visually compare the completed stride-1 `sample_02` control with stride-3 `sample_02`; (2) finish the internal processing-time/GPU comparison for `sample_02`–`sample_05`; (3) if the control confirms cadence is the regression, make the v4 response/hysteresis time-aware so stride is a compute knob rather than changing the cameraman's real-time behaviour; (4) rerun the same multi-sample set before re-declaring stride 3 accepted.

## OEV zero-copy option 2 — shared CUDA/Vulkan buffers integrated; real stereo render correct; A/B measured (15 Aug 2026)

**Outcome: SUCCESS on a feature branch; production remains unchanged.** Linux decode now uses CUDA-VMM allocations imported by Vulkan as `VkBuffer` copy sources, one CUDA-signalled/Vulkan-waited binary semaphore per camera/slot, and one GPU-only buffer-to-ordinary-`VramPool`-texture submission for all four Y/UV planes. Existing render, replay, wgpu-detection fallback, lookahead and immediate-mode paths consume ordinary textures. CUDA detection still consumes the original shared-buffer pointers. There is no CPU pixel round-trip between NVDEC and the normal render textures.

**Exact branches/SHAs:**
- `JhnsonO/video-stitcher` branch `feature/linux-cuda-buffer-zero-copy`, head `61aced9687d2c441c48d11e29d3aa28df18b3beb` (implementation `f7e4dce9`, rustfmt `a055bb17`, corrected committed lock graph `61aced96`), based on production `53fe10f548d5767ad94ef66aeaedf2d8c7161f27`.
- `JhnsonO/wgpu` branch `feature/cuda-buffer-semaphore`, head `d74e00f2415e55c0f09a87b0497d66d8192a44bb`: minimal queue-wait backport parent `62e15ce1` plus `VK_KHR_external_semaphore_fd` adapter enablement. No old shared-image diagnostics are in this branch.
- `JhnsonO/ffa-automations` branch `agent/zc-buffer-copy-proof`; clean integration/control harness evidence is committed through `a20eedd3fca8562f886383fe585b4d2e87de340c`. No production workflow, Reco pin, network-volume binary/manifest, or `--no-zero-copy` setting was changed.

**Implementation scope (14 files, compile-gated against the production base):** workspace `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock`; `reco-core` CUDA/Vulkan/zero-copy interop, session setup/frame processing/buffering/detection/VRAM pool; and `reco-io` SmartFileSource/decode threads. Linux NVDEC copies Y+UV to shared buffers, synchronizes, signals the slot semaphore, and sends the slot. Vulkan stages both camera waits at `TRANSFER`, copies left/right Y+UV into the acquired normal texture slot, and polls before binary-semaphore/slot reuse. Start-time skipped frames consume their semaphore signals with an empty waited submission. Both double-buffer slots, immediate one-slot texture pooling, lookahead lifetime, P010 byte widths, and error-path slot ownership are covered. Other OS paths and the CPU fallback remain present.

**Phase-1 destination-texture proof — VERIFIED byte-exact:** diagnostic Reco `acdcc61ece2f1d9bda453dea32ec3be10d34172a`; compile run `31887205847` / artifact `9247964269`; real fixture run `31888822303`, attempt 2 job `95022628358`, artifact `9248117131`, RTX 4090 / driver `570.195.03`. CUDA readback contained real football data (left Y mean 89.51, right 76.65); all four ordinary destination planes matched the CUDA dumps byte-for-byte; deterministic sentinels at offsets 0 / 4,147,200 / 8,290,560 matched in CUDA and the normal destination texture. The 180-frame render completed with normal football imagery and no green output. Wall-clock: 20.2s / 8.9 fps; session: 17.4s / 10.4 fps. Pod duration 281.5s; approximate cost $0.0579.

**Clean compile proof — VERIFIED:** run `31890863984`, job `95026811217`, artifact `9248925986`. Exact 14-file scope, unique wgpu quartet at `d74e00f2`, rustfmt, locked release `cargo check -p reco-cli --features cuda`, and targeted `nv12_and_p010_plane_pitches_are_copy_aligned` test all passed (1 passed, 0 failed). The two prior clean-gate attempts were not compiler evidence: `31890356726` stopped on rustfmt; `31890484385` stopped on a stale `reco-io -> ash` lock edge. Each was corrected independently before this pass.

**Clean real integration — VERIFIED:**
- Run `31892619238`, job `95031060826`, artifact `9249035815`, RTX 2000 Ada / driver `570.172.08`: start-time `0.2` + `lookahead=0` immediate smoke passed, including synchronized buffer copy and `GpuResident detection: CUDA path (TensorRT/ORT-CUDA)`. A 180-frame buffered stereo render completed at 3.3 fps wall-clock / 3.6 session fps. Four independently extracted checkpoints (frames 0/60/120/179) show normal football imagery, correct colour and no green/corruption. The workflow's final red status was only a false-negative grep for an immediate-only detection log at the separate buffered `detect_and_track_only` call site. Pod cost: approximately $0.0255.
- Run `31893154739`, attempt 2 job `95032753193`, artifact `9249237633`, NVIDIA L4 / driver `570.195.03`: immediate ownership/sync/CUDA-detection smoke passed; the clean 180-frame render passed at 41.4s / 4.4 fps wall-clock (36.8s / 4.9 session fps). Reported averages: shared-buffer wait+copy staging 1.7ms, stitch 0.1ms, readback 0.3ms, submit 0.1ms, encode 0.8ms. The identical same-pod `--no-zero-copy` workload completed 180 frames at 59.4s / 3.0 fps wall-clock (53.2s / 3.4 session fps): the new path was about 1.47x faster wall-clock. Buffered telemetry does not include lookahead-produce detection time; wall-clock is authoritative. Immediate telemetry measured CUDA-resident detection at 206.7ms average on this L4 and identified detection as the bottleneck. Approximate pod cost: $0.0535.
- The L4 baseline process exited 139 only after writing all 180 frames and its session summary. A final same-host regression control, run `31894036556`, job `95034455445`, artifact `9249368772`, RTX 4090 / driver `570.195.03`, compared the clean branch with exact production base `53fe10f5`. Both produced byte-count-valid 180-frame `--no-zero-copy` outputs at the same 3.9 fps wall-clock / 4.4 session fps. The clean branch exited 0; production base exited 139 with the CUDA-context teardown fingerprint. Therefore the option-2 branch did **not** regress the fallback and avoided the pre-existing/host-sensitive teardown failure in this control. Approximate cost: $0.0767.

**Performance interpretation:** on the same L4 workload the new path improves 3.0 -> 4.4 fps wall-clock (+47%). The RTX 4090 phase-1 render measured 8.9 wall-clock / 10.4 session fps versus the historical representative `--no-zero-copy` ~5.8 fps, but that comparison is cross-run. It remains below the historical ~19 fps corrupt direct-image path. The deliberate per-frame Vulkan completion poll makes binary-semaphore/slot reuse safe but is a likely throughput ceiling; do not remove it without a reverse completion signal or equivalent ownership proof.

**Remaining risks / recommendation:** the real fixtures exercised 8-bit NV12; P010 sizing/alignment compiled and unit-tested (3840-wide Y and UV rows are both 7,680 bytes) but still needs one real 10-bit fixture. macOS/Windows paths were cfg-isolated but not runtime-tested here. Recommend opening the implementation for review and running one P010 fixture plus, if a production-performance decision needs it, one clean-branch 4090 same-pod A/B. The architecture is viable and visually correct; do not return to shared-`VkImage` experiments. Do not merge or change the production Reco pin without Johnson's approval.

## OEV zero-copy NV12 corruption — EXP8 allocation-size A/B FALSIFIED; stop-loss reached (15 Aug 2026)

**Single-variable test:** `video-stitcher@58542bbb9b91560e533ccc846a5eb3bd6e9d9db4` (branch `diag/zc-exp8-image-alloc-size`, based directly on Avenue-2 `e810a04e`) changes only Vulkan image import `allocation_size(shared_mem.alloc_size as u64)` -> `allocation_size(mem_reqs.size)`.

**Valid hardware run:** ffa run `31876438503`, job `94992772328`, artifact `9244919570`, RTX 4090 / driver `570.195.03`. Hardened selection required Vulkan, CUDA init, NVDEC and ORT CUDA all PASS before accepting the pod. Reco built successfully and the real GoPro fixture decoded frame 0.

**Controls:** CUDA frame-0 data remained correct (`left Y mean=89.51`, `right Y mean=76.65`). The deterministic CUDA sentinel at offsets `0i`, `4,147,200`, `8,290,560` was present byte-exact in CUDA. The native Vulkan/wgpu readback control also passed byte-exact.

**Decisive result:** despite exact Vulkan allocation sizing, all imported image source planes remained all-zero (`left_y_vram_src 0/8,294,400`, `left_uv_vram_src 0/4,147,200`, right Y/UV similarly zero), and Vulkan saw `0/1024` sentinel bytes at all three offsets. **Allocation-size mismatch is falsified.**

**Infra notes:** compile-only run `31874496979` timed out during `cargo update` before Rust compilation and is not hypothesis evidence. The first two fixture attempts under `31875839603` had broken NVDEC (`CUDA_ERROR_NO_DEVICE`) and were inconclusive. The branch-only launcher was then hardened to reject NVDEC-broken hosts before download/build; attempt 3 above is the valid result.

**Evidence boundary / stop-loss:** CUDA producer, readback, wgpu initial-state/layout, semaphore sync, generic CUDA-VMM -> Vulkan OPAQUE_FD sharing (VkBuffer), external-image capability/dedicated-allocation checks, and allocation sizing have all been addressed. The unresolved defect is image-specific to the CUDA-created allocation being used as an imported linear `VkImage`. Stop parameter-by-parameter tweaking of that path absent new external evidence.

**Next bounded architecture:** CUDA/NVDEC -> shared external `VkBuffer` Y/UV -> CUDA signals external semaphore -> Vulkan waits -> GPU buffer-to-normal-texture copy -> existing render/tracking. First prove one Y plane byte-exact on a diagnostic branch, then a short real GoPro render before integrating Y+UV/double-buffering. No EXP8 Reco change is merged to production.

## OEV zero-copy NV12 corruption — EXP7: generic CUDA↔Vulkan OPAQUE_FD sharing PASSES; failure is image-specific — real hardware, decisive (15 Aug 2026)

**Correction to the immediately-following Avenue-2 entry:** run `31854089581` proved that the synchronized Vulkan **image** path did not observe a CUDA-written sentinel that CUDA itself read back byte-exact. It did **not** prove that CUDA and Vulkan cannot share the same backing allocation in general. EXP7 directly tests CUDA-VMM-export -> Vulkan-import with a `VkBuffer` and passes byte-exact, so the proven failure boundary is now specifically the imported **VkImage** path.

**Compile proof:** `JhnsonO/video-stitcher@9059470f01065d5336af8c94bac27a860156dfec` (`diag/zc-exp7-external-memory-caps`) compiled against `JhnsonO/wgpu@c8b6f2f00895210857f77f2a10fc1a32a80d5148` in run `31871577622`. Exact compile-proven sources: `vulkan.rs` 1476 lines / SHA-256 `4da792382d954f5ffe68865d5ae84db9e778c2f6e452917a7749149f50c41089`; `cuda.rs` 1312 lines / SHA-256 `e81e693071608caef213eb34e68335d064e3aded6c58214147f2b6be4ac303b7`.

**Real EXP7 run:** `31872745393`, `ffa-automations` branch `diag/zc-exp7-real-fixture`, head `3d51d19c414638c11f66d1a57d82705bef56f6d8`. NVIDIA preflight passed, Reco SHA `9059470f...`, wgpu resolution gate passed, EXP7 exited after two shared-texture probes before decode, artifact `oev-zero-copy-diag-31872745393` captured the evidence, and RunPod termination was confirmed.

**VkBuffer control: PASS byte-exact.** Vulkan 1.3.275. External-buffer capabilities for `TRANSFER_SRC|TRANSFER_DST + OPAQUE_FD`: `EXPORTABLE=true`, `IMPORTABLE=true`, `DEDICATED_ONLY=false`, OPAQUE_FD compatible. CUDA wrote the deterministic 1024-byte sentinel at offsets 0 / 524288 / 1047552, CUDA ground truth was byte-exact, CUDA signalled the external semaphore after synchronization, and the exact Vulkan transfer waited on it at `TRANSFER`. Vulkan returned `byte_exact=true` at all three offsets; `ZC_EXP7_BUF_ALIAS_RESULT=PASS`.

**Image capability queries: no obvious compatibility/dedicated-allocation red flag.**
- Y `R8_UNORM` LINEAR 3840x2160: `IMPORTABLE=true`, `DEDICATED_ONLY=false`, OPAQUE_FD compatible, `requiresDedicatedAllocation=false`, `prefersDedicatedAllocation=false`; Vulkan requirement 8,294,400 bytes / alignment 128 / `memoryTypeBits=0xf`; CUDA allocation 8,388,608.
- UV `R8G8_UNORM` LINEAR 1920x1080: same flags; Vulkan requirement 4,147,200 bytes / alignment 128 / `memoryTypeBits=0xf`; CUDA allocation 4,194,304.
`ZC_DIAG` still reports row pitch 3840, subresource offset 0, subresource size exactly pitch*height, and allocation size >= requirements. `EXP7_IMAGE_CAPABILITY_RED_FLAG=NO`.

**Important API correction:** do not use `vkGetMemoryFdPropertiesKHR` with `VK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY_HANDLE_TYPE_OPAQUE_FD_BIT`; VUID-00674 forbids it. The temporary draft using that query was removed before compile/hardware testing.

**Now ruled out unless contradictory evidence appears:** readback-mechanism failure; wgpu UNINITIALIZED/layout discard; missing CUDA->Vulkan semaphore synchronization; generic CUDA-VMM -> Vulkan OPAQUE_FD sharing failure; obvious external-image capability rejection / OPAQUE_FD incompatibility / dedicated-allocation requirement.

**Remaining failure boundary:** imported **linear VkImage** representation/binding/visibility semantics. One measured difference, not yet a root-cause claim, is allocation sizing: production imports CUDA's VMM granularity-rounded sizes (8,388,608 / 4,194,304) while Vulkan image requirements are smaller exact sizes (8,294,400 / 4,147,200). This is a bounded next experiment candidate, not a conclusion.

**Stop point:** EXP7 recorded; no production merge and no next hypothesis started.

## OEV zero-copy NV12 corruption — Avenue 1 CLOSED (readback mechanism sound), Avenue 2 FALSIFIED (physical aliasing does NOT hold) — real hardware evidence, decisive (15 Aug 2026)

**Ticket context:** continuation of the zero-copy NV12 corruption thread (entries below). Both prior hypotheses (A: wgpu layout-discard, B: missing CUDA->Vulkan semaphore sync) were already falsified with real post-wait pixel evidence -- CUDA writes proven correct (mean=89.51/76.65), Vulkan-side readback of the same imported textures still all-zero. This entry runs the two-avenue search order from that point: (1) is the ZC_EXP4 readback mechanism itself trustworthy, and if so (2) do CUDA and Vulkan actually alias the same physical memory.

**Avenue 1 -- ZC_EXP4 readback mechanism, validated. CLOSED, PASS.**
Built (diagnostic only): `JhnsonO/video-stitcher@a6f12c06` (branch `diag/cuda-vulkan-semaphore`) adds `zc_exp6_native_readback_control()` in `session/frame_processing.rs` -- two plain wgpu-native R8Unorm 256x64 textures (never imported/shared), same device/queue, same call site as the real ZC_EXP4 readbacks: (A) `write_texture` an `i%256` pattern -> direct readback via the exact same `zc_exp4_readback_texture()` helper; (B) same pattern routed through `copy_texture_to_texture` (one encoder, one submit, one `device.poll(wait_indefinitely())` -- mirroring `VramPool::copy_from_textures()`'s shape) -> readback of the destination.
Run `31852807648` (real hardware, RTX 4090, success): both sub-controls came back **PASS byte-exact** against the expected pattern (16384/16384 bytes each). Same run, same frame: all 8 imported-plane ZC_EXP4 readbacks (left/right x Y/UV x src/dst) still all-zero, unchanged from every prior cycle.
**Conclusion: the readback measurement itself is proven sound.** The zeros reported in every prior cycle are genuine measurements of the imported textures' actual content, not an artifact of `copy_texture_to_buffer`/`map_async`. This closes the one standing question that could have overturned hypotheses A and B -- it does not; they remain falsified.

**Avenue 2 -- CUDA<->Vulkan physical memory aliasing, tested directly. FALSIFIED -- aliasing does NOT hold.**
Built (diagnostic only): `JhnsonO/video-stitcher@b33827ef` + `e810a04e` (same branch). `crates/reco-io/src/zero_copy.rs`: left decode thread, frame 0 only, immediately after the real frame's `cuCtxSynchronize()` confirms the genuine NVDEC->shared-texture copy is complete, writes a deterministic sentinel (`(j as u8) ^ 0xC3`, 1024 bytes) via `cuda_memcpy_htod_2d` at three byte offsets in the Y plane: row 0, row 1080 (middle), row 2159 (last), using the slot's real `y_ptr`/`y_pitch`. `crates/reco-core/src/interop/vulkan.rs`: `ZC_DIAG` now also logs `vk_subresource_offset`/`vk_subresource_size` from `get_image_subresource_layout` (both came back `offset=0`, `size` exactly `pitch*height` -- confirms the CUDA device-pointer byte offset and the Vulkan subresource byte offset are the same addressing scheme, so the sentinel offsets are directly comparable across both readback paths with no translation needed).
First dispatch (run `31853736722`) landed on a bad pod (`cuvidGetDecoderCaps -> CUDA_ERROR_NO_DEVICE`, NVDEC smoke test printed FAIL but the workflow did not abort) -- decode never reached frame 0, no diagnostic evidence collected, experiment discarded as invalid rather than interpreted.
Retry, run `31854089581` (real hardware, RTX 4090, `NVDEC=PASS`, success) -- **the decisive test:**
- CUDA-side ground truth (independent DtoH readback of the exact same slot, same offsets): sentinel **PRESENT, byte-exact** at all 3 offsets (row 0, row 1080, row 2159). Confirms the HtoD sentinel write itself landed correctly in the CUDA-visible allocation.
- Vulkan-side readback (`left_y_vram_src`, same frame, same offsets, translated via the identical addressing scheme confirmed by the subresource logging above): sentinel **ABSENT** at all 3 offsets -- `nonzero=0/1024` at every one, i.e. all-zero, not corrupted/shifted/partial.
- Same run's full-plane `left DIAG frame0 Y-plane readback`: CUDA side `min=0 max=255 mean=89.51 nonzero_bytes=8283562/8294400` (correct real frame + the 3 sentinel patches, as expected). Vulkan side (`ZC_EXP4: left_y_vram_src`): `min=0 max=0 mean=0.00 nonzero_bytes=0/8294400` -- the *entire* plane is zero, not just failing to show the sentinel.

**Conclusion: CUDA and Vulkan are NOT reading the same physical backing pages.** This is a directly-observed negative result (a known-good write, confirmed present on the CUDA side, is completely absent on the Vulkan side at byte-exact matching offsets), not an inference from allocation-size/pitch/handle-success logging. Per the ticket's own decision rule: **stop chasing rendering/wgpu-side hypotheses (already exhausted: A, B). The failure boundary is the external-memory import/bind step itself** -- `vkAllocateMemory` + `ImportMemoryFdInfoKHR` + `vkBindImageMemory` in `interop/vulkan.rs::create_shared_texture()` is allocating/binding memory that Vulkan considers valid (API calls all succeed, `ZC_DIAG` PASS on size/alignment/subresource) but which is not actually backed by the same pages CUDA wrote to.

**Ruled out by the evidence above (do not re-open without new contradictory evidence):**
- Readback/measurement infrastructure (avenue 1, this entry).
- wgpu imported-texture layout/state discard (hypothesis A, prior entry).
- Missing CUDA->Vulkan cross-API synchronization (hypothesis B, prior entry).
- Gross pitch/alignment/allocation-size mismatch (`ZC_DIAG` has shown PASS on both since before this entry, and subresource offset/size now also confirmed identity).

**Next avenue (per ticket's own search order, avenue 3 -- not yet started, no new hypothesis chosen without discussion):** external-memory import/bind semantics at the Vulkan/CUDA/driver level. Concrete candidates, none yet tested:
1. **Handle-type/export-path mismatch.** CUDA side allocates via `cuMemCreate` + `cuMemExportToShareableHandle` (VMM API, `allocate_shared_memory()` in `interop/cuda.rs`) and exports an fd; Vulkan imports it as `VK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY_HANDLE_TYPE_OPAQUE_FD_BIT`. NVIDIA's documented/most-tested zero-copy interop direction is typically Vulkan-allocates-exports, CUDA-imports (the reverse of what this codebase does). Worth explicitly checking whether `cuMemExportToShareableHandle`'s fd is genuinely spec-compliant `OPAQUE_FD` as far as `vkAllocateMemory`'s importer is concerned, vs. merely accepted without error.
2. **Missing `VkMemoryDedicatedAllocateInfo`.** The current `vkAllocateMemory` call in `create_shared_texture()` does not request a dedicated allocation. If the driver's exported CUDA allocation requires (or was implicitly created as) a dedicated allocation, importing it into a suballocated/non-dedicated Vulkan `VkDeviceMemory` can succeed at the API level while not actually sharing pages.
3. **Query actual compatibility instead of trusting success codes.** Neither `vkGetPhysicalDeviceExternalBufferProperties`/`ImageFormatProperties2` with `VkPhysicalDeviceExternalImageFormatInfo` nor CUDA's own external-memory capability query has been called anywhere in this codebase -- the current code goes straight to allocate/import/bind and trusts that success means compatibility. This is the one item explicitly flagged by the ticket as necessary before trusting any success code as proof of real interop.

**CURRENT SINGLE ACTIVE NEXT ACTION:** none chosen yet -- per standing instruction, avenue 3's three candidates above are not to be started without discussing with Johnson first. Recommendation: start with #3 (capability query) since it's read-only/low-risk and would definitively confirm or rule out #1/#2 rather than guessing.
## OEV zero-copy NV12 corruption — Ticket 2 (CUDA<->Vulkan external semaphore) IMPLEMENTED, DEBUGGED THROUGH 2 REAL BUGS — hypothesis B genuinely falsified with post-wait destination evidence (14 Aug 2026, corrected same day)

**Correction to this entry's original conclusion:** the "falsified" call below was first made off the **second real fixture attempt** (run `31849128990`), which only re-ran the pre-existing ZC_EXP4 **source** readback (`left_y_vram_src` etc.) — a readback positioned in code *before* `add_wait_semaphore()`/`copy_from_textures()` even ran. That is PRE-wait evidence; it only proves "Vulkan sees zero before consuming the wait," not "Vulkan still sees zero after consuming the wait." Johnson flagged this gap directly and it was correct to flag — the conclusion was premature. See below for the actual decisive test.

**Ticket context:** continuation of the entry further below. Hypothesis A (wgpu layout-discard) was falsified there. This entry covers hypothesis B: missing CUDA->Vulkan cross-API memory-visibility sync, fixed via a real binary semaphore per double-buffer slot (4 total: left/right x slot 0/1, Y+UV sharing one semaphore per slot per the slot-lifecycle evidence already on record).

**Built (diagnostic only, no production merge):**
- `JhnsonO/wgpu@c8b6f2f00895210857f77f2a10fc1a32a80d5148` (branch `diag/cuda-vulkan-semaphore-ext`, on top of the Ticket-1 `0750ea8` base): enables `VK_KHR_external_semaphore` (version-gated `< API_VERSION_1_1`) + `VK_KHR_external_semaphore_fd` as optional device extensions. Real, load-bearing gap found by grep: this fork (like upstream wgpu) never requested either, so `vkGetSemaphoreFdKHR` would have had no function pointer loaded even though `add_wait_semaphore`/`add_signal_semaphore` (Ticket 1's earlier backport) already existed in wgpu-hal. **This is also the SHA that must be used as `EXPECTED_WGPU_REV`** — the diag remote script briefly pinned the older `0750ea8` (missing this extension enablement) and was corrected same session.
- `JhnsonO/video-stitcher@024ce664e1abc1b2223e04951f6797cc52c2e85c` (branch `diag/cuda-vulkan-semaphore`, on top of the Ticket-1 `1361770` base): `interop/vulkan.rs::create_export_semaphore`; `interop/cuda.rs` adds `cuImportExternalSemaphore`/`cuSignalExternalSemaphoresAsync`/`cuDestroyExternalSemaphore` FFI; `GpuBufInfo`/`SharedTextureSet` carry the 4 semaphores end to end; decode thread signals after `cuCtxSynchronize()`; `copy_to_vram_pool_platform` waits via `gpu.queue.as_hal::<Vulkan>().add_wait_semaphore(...)` immediately before `copy_from_textures()`.
- `JhnsonO/video-stitcher@1ccc4694e5e776a4e94dbd1acf55eda2965b94c2` (same branch, on top of `024ce66`): one-line fix, adds `wgpu::TextureUsages::COPY_SRC` to `VramPool`'s destination texture usage flags (`session/vram_pool.rs`). Without this the existing post-copy ZC_EXP4 destination readback (`left_y_vram_dst` etc.) panics on `copy_texture_to_buffer`, which is why no post-wait pixel evidence had ever actually been collected before this fix.

**Static trace confirming the wait IS consumed by the correct submission (resolves this entry's previously-open question):**
- `wgpu@c8b6f2f`, `wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/mod.rs`: `add_wait_semaphore()` pushes onto `Queue.wait_semaphores`, a queue-global `Mutex<SemaphoreList>` (not bound to any specific command buffer). `Queue::submit()` drains that guard via `SemaphoreList::append()` (confirmed via `Vec::append`, which empties the source) into whichever submission runs next.
- `video-stitcher@024ce66`, `session/vram_pool.rs::copy_from_textures()`: encodes all 4 texture copies into one `CommandEncoder`, exactly one `gpu.queue.submit()` call.
- `frame_processing.rs::copy_to_vram_pool_platform()`: the two `add_wait_semaphore()` calls (left/right slot) happen immediately before `pool.copy_from_textures()`, nothing else touches `gpu.queue` in between on this call path. **Confirmed: the staged waits are consumed by the exact submission that performs the shared-texture copy**, not orphaned or consumed by an unrelated submit. (Not exhaustively ruled out across other threads touching the same `gpu.queue` concurrently — flagged as an assumption, not verified.)

**Three real bugs found and fixed via actual dispatches/inspection, not by inference alone:**
1. Compile-proof (`ffa-automations` branch `diag/cuda-vulkan-semaphore-compile-proof`, workflow `oev-wgpu-hal-lockfile-gate.yml`): `error[E0599]: no method named get_semaphore_fd_khr` — ash 0.38.0+1.3.281 drops the `_khr` suffix. Fixed (`30c7eb2`), **real PASS** (run `31847812445`).
2. Real GoPro fixture, first attempt (run `31848571928`): `CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_CONTEXT` in `cuImportExternalSemaphore`, called before any decode thread established a CUDA context there. Fixed with `cuda_ensure_context()?` (`024ce66`).
3. Missing `COPY_SRC` on `VramPool` destination textures (this session): the post-wait destination readback panicked every time it was reached, so no decisive evidence had ever actually been collected despite two "real fixture" runs. Fixed (`1ccc4694`).

**Decisive post-wait test (run `31851072187`, `RECO_SHA=1ccc4694`, `EXPECTED_WGPU_REV=c8b6f2f`) — the actual acceptance test:**
- No panic, no CUDA error, `GATE PASS` confirms unified wgpu quartet from `JhnsonO/wgpu@c8b6f2f`.
- CUDA-side control readback (independent of Vulkan): left mean=89.51, right mean=76.65 — correct, as always.
- ZC_EXP4 source (pre-wait) readback: all zero on all 4 planes — unchanged from every prior cycle, as expected (this readback is upstream of the wait, its zero result was never the question).
- **ZC_EXP4 destination (post-wait, post-`copy_from_textures()`, post-`device.poll(wait_indefinitely())`) readback — the actually decisive evidence: all zero on all 4 planes** (`left_y_vram_dst`/`left_uv_vram_dst`/`right_y_vram_dst`/`right_uv_vram_dst`, every one `min=0 max=0 mean=0.00 nonzero_bytes=0/N`). The readback mechanism itself is proven sound here (it executed cleanly, returned a real numeric result, not a panic or error) — this is a genuine zero, not a broken measurement.

**Conclusion: hypothesis B (missing CUDA->Vulkan semaphore sync) is now genuinely falsified**, on the correct post-wait evidentiary basis this time: the wait is staged correctly, consumed by the correct submission (proven by static trace above), GPU completion is proven (`device.poll(wait_indefinitely())` returns, no error), and pixel data downstream of all of that is still all-zero. Both credible hypotheses on record (A: layout-discard, B: missing cross-API sync) are now closed with real post-fix hardware evidence, not premature or PRE-wait evidence.

**Next 2-3 evidence-backed avenues (not yet chosen, no new hypothesis started without discussion — per Johnson's standing instruction):**
1. **Readback mechanism itself, isolated.** ZC_EXP4's `copy_texture_to_buffer` + `map_async` path has never been validated against a texture independently known to contain real data (e.g. a Vulkan-native render target written by a Vulkan draw call, not an imported/shared one). *Effort: low* (one throwaway texture + one compute/clear pass in the same diagnostic harness). *Risk: low* — if this also reads zero, it indicts the readback path itself rather than the interop, which would overturn both "falsified" conclusions on this file. Worth running first since it's cheap and could invalidate a lot of prior work if wrong.
2. **Physical memory identity check.** Confirm the CUDA-written device pointer and the Vulkan-imported `VkDeviceMemory` genuinely alias the same physical allocation — e.g. by having CUDA write a distinctive non-zero pattern at a known byte offset and reading that exact offset from the Vulkan side via the same proven-working readback, rather than trusting the pitch/alignment log lines (`ZC_DIAG: ... PASS_vs_mem_reqs=PASS`) as sufficient proof of aliasing. *Effort: medium* (new diagnostic write-then-read probe). *Risk: medium* — plausible given nothing else has explained the zero, but the file's `ZC_DIAG` lines already showed size/alignment matching, so this narrows a specific remaining gap rather than reopening a closed question.
3. **Driver/environment-specific external-memory behavior.** Some NVIDIA driver + WSL/container combinations are known to silently fail `VK_KHR_external_memory_fd` imports (memory "imports" successfully at the API level but the backing pages are never actually shared) rather than erroring. Check driver version and confirm via `vkGetMemoryFdPropertiesKHR` or a minimal standalone CUDA/Vulkan interop sample outside this codebase entirely. *Effort: medium-high* (environment archaeology, possibly a from-scratch minimal repro outside `reco-cli`). *Risk: low-medium* — this would explain a "everything is wired correctly per the API contract, yet nothing happens" result cleanly, but requires stepping outside the current codebase to test in isolation.

**CURRENT SINGLE ACTIVE NEXT ACTION:** none chosen yet. Do not default to avenue 1/2/3 above without discussing with Johnson first — recommend avenue 1 (readback mechanism isolation) as the cheapest and most consequential to run next, since it could overturn prior falsifications if it fails, but this is a recommendation, not a queued task.

## OEV zero-copy NV12 corruption — ZC_EXP5 (initial_state/layout-discard) FALSIFIED with real evidence; slot lifecycle confirmed, resuming external-semaphore design (14 Aug 2026)

**Ticket context:** continuation of the "zero-copy NV12 corruption" thread above (12 Aug). CUDA-side shared-memory writes were already proven correct (cycle 4/5); Vulkan's own readback of the same shared memory (ZC_EXP4, cycle 6, commit `79667aac`) came back all-zero. Two competing hypotheses were scoped: (A) wgpu's imported-texture layout/state tracking discards the CUDA-written contents on first use (cheap to test), (B) missing CUDA→Vulkan cross-API synchronization (external semaphores — larger, was Ticket 2's original scope, paused to test A first).

**Hypothesis A tested and FALSIFIED.** Real upstream match: `gfx-rs/wgpu@6208d80934e996e60984ff2b2a88b9cae639c7b6` (PR #9496, "Add an explicit `initial_state` to `create_texture_from_hal`") — upstream's own description states the prior hard-coded `UNINITIALIZED` tracker state permits a content-discarding Vulkan transition and specifically affected zero-copy hardware-decoded video imports. Extremely close match to our symptom, worth testing despite not being our exact NVIDIA interop path.

**Built and verified (compile, then real hardware):**
- `JhnsonO/wgpu`, branch `backport/9496-initial-state`, commit `0750ea88863e6e4e8acd9128ce820d71f44e968f` (2 commits on top of the existing Ticket-1 `62e15ce1` base): backported `initial_state: wgt::TextureUses` through all 4 real call-chain layers (`wgpu-core/src/device/resource.rs`, `wgpu-core/src/device/global.rs`, `wgpu/src/backend/wgpu_core.rs`, `wgpu/src/api/device.rs`), plus a small `vulkan::Queue::family_index()` accessor (mirrors existing `as_raw()`) needed for the Reco-side barrier submission.
- `JhnsonO/video-stitcher`, branch `diag/wgpu-initial-state-backport`, commit `1361770abd334eeb48799bd812bb4092968af72a` (on top of proven base `d44df777`): `Cargo.toml` quartet repointed at the new fork commit; `interop/vulkan.rs::create_shared_texture` gets a one-time `PREINITIALIZED -> TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL` VkImage layout transition (submitted synchronously, blocking on a Vulkan fence, on the same raw queue wgpu itself uses) immediately after `vkBindImageMemory` and before wgpu ever wraps the image, plus `initial_state = COPY_SRC` passed to `create_texture_from_hal` (matches the texture's real first use — `copy_texture_to_buffer`/`copy_texture_to_texture` as source, confirmed by direct inspection, never sampled or copy-dst'd first). **Real compile bug caught and fixed in the same commit lineage:** the initial diff only touched `vulkan.rs`; a proper `git grep` across the full tree found 3 other real call sites of `create_texture_from_hal` (`dmabuf.rs` — Linux, actually broke the build; `d3d11.rs`/`metal.rs` — Windows/macOS, cfg-gated out of this build but were left broken) — all 3 fixed by passing `wgpu::TextureUses::UNINITIALIZED` (exact prior behavior, zero change, not investigated for the same bug — out of scope).
- Compile-proof (`ffa-automations` branch `diag/wgpu-initial-state-compile-proof`, workflow `oev-wgpu-hal-lockfile-gate.yml`, reused the exact proven `cargo update -p wgpu --precise 28.0.1` + hard quartet-resolution-gate mechanics from the 1d run): **PASSED for real** — run `31841623350`, `cargo_check_exit_code=0`, `cargo check: PASS`, all four wgpu-family crates unified from the new fork SHA, verified from the actual log text, not job-green alone. Two earlier attempts on this same gate genuinely failed first (compile-log-path bug lost the real error on attempt 1; the dmabuf.rs/d3d11.rs/metal.rs gap was the real error surfaced by attempt 2) — both real, both fixed, not noise.

**Real GoPro HEVC fixture dispatch** (`ffa-automations` branch `diag/zc-exp5-real-fixture`, workflow `oev-zero-copy-diag.yml`, `RECO_SHA` pointed at `1361770...`, added the same proven `cargo update`+gate to this script too since its Cargo.toml now patches wgpu for the first time in this script's history — without it the run would've failed on the already-solved crates.io-vs-fork split instead of testing anything new): run `31842779824`, completed.

**Result — clean, unambiguous, independently verified:**
- CUDA-side readback: correct, real football frame (`mean=89.51`/`76.65`, `nonzero_bytes≈8.29M/8.29M`) — known-good baseline, unaffected.
- ZC_EXP5 barrier: fired **exactly 8x** (left/right × Y/UV × double-buffer), confirmed via log — and per the code's blocking fence-wait, that means genuinely GPU-executed and complete before any CUDA write, not merely recorded.
- GPU confirmed real: `NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090 (Vulkan)`, not llvmpipe.
- **ZC_EXP4 Vulkan-side source readback: still all-zero on all 4 planes** (`left_y_vram_src`, `left_uv_vram_src`, `right_y_vram_src`, `right_uv_vram_src` — every one `min=0 max=0 mean=0.00 nonzero_bytes=0/N`). Cross-checked independently at the byte level (not just the log line) via the raw dumped `.raw` files — `python3` byte-count confirms `0 nonzero` out of `8294400` bytes on `left_y_vram_src`, matches exactly.
- One unrelated finding, doesn't affect the above: a `wgpu` validation panic fired *after* all four decisive readback lines were already captured, on a *different* texture (`vram_L_Y_0`, the VramPool's own destination texture, not the CUDA-shared source texture this ticket touched) — missing `COPY_SRC` in its creation usage flags, tripped by the diagnostic's destination-readback half (added cycle 6, apparently never actually exercised until this run reached it). Pre-existing, not caused by ZC_EXP5, doesn't invalidate the source-readback evidence above (all captured before the panic). Small standalone fix if that second-half diagnostic is ever relied on again — not scheduled.

**Conclusion: hypothesis A is falsified.** Changing the Vulkan image's tracked/actual layout state made zero difference — Vulkan still sees literally zero bytes where CUDA has a complete, correct frame. This rules out "wgpu is discarding the imported texture's contents" as the mechanism, cleanly and with real hardware evidence, not by inference from documentation. What remains is a genuine cross-API memory-visibility gap: CUDA finishes writing (proven, `cuCtxSynchronize()` confirms host-side completion) but nothing establishes the GPU-side memory dependency Vulkan needs to actually *see* that write — exactly the class of problem external semaphores exist to solve, and exactly Ticket 2's original (pre-A-detour) scope.

**Slot lifecycle confirmed with evidence, not assumed** (needed to choose binary-vs-timeline semaphore correctly before implementation):
- Traced the real call graph: `run_loop.rs::produce_one` calls `copy_to_vram_pool` (which internally blocks on `device.poll(wait_indefinitely())` after `copy_from_textures`) **then** `detect_and_track_only` (CPU/GPU detection reading the already-copied VramPool destination texture) **then, only after both**, `release_gpu_decode_slot(&frame)` — which is the only thing that sends the slot index back to `left_slot_free_tx`/`right_slot_free_tx`.
- The decode thread (`spawn_single_decoder_gpu`) blocks on `slot_free_rx.recv_timeout(100ms)` before writing any new frame into a given slot — it structurally cannot start the next `cuMemcpy2D` into slot N until the render side's full consumption of the previous slot-N frame (GPU copy + CPU detection) is confirmed complete.
- The left/right pairing thread (`spawn_decode_threads_gpu`) does a plain blocking `recv()`/`send()` pair-up with no drop/resync logic anywhere in steady state (temporal alignment is a one-time startup skip, not an ongoing mechanism); the frame-ready channel is a bounded `sync_channel(1)` that blocks producers rather than dropping frames under backpressure.
- **Conclusion: both preconditions for a binary semaphore per slot are satisfied by the real code** — (P1) slot recycle is fully gated on confirmed consumer completion, not merely on encoding/submission; (P2) no code path drops a frame between a would-be signal and its matching wait. **Binary semaphore per slot is the evidence-backed choice, not timeline.** 4 independent slots need their own semaphore: left-slot-0, left-slot-1, right-slot-0, right-slot-1 (Y+UV share one semaphore per slot — both written by the same decode-thread iteration before any signal, both read together by the same `copy_from_textures` call).

**CURRENT SINGLE ACTIVE NEXT ACTION:** implement the diagnostic-only CUDA→Vulkan external semaphore synchronization per Ticket 1's original design (semaphore creation/ownership, CUDA import via `cuImportExternalSemaphore`/`cuSignalExternalSemaphoresAsync`, signal immediately after the existing `cuCtxSynchronize()` in `spawn_single_decoder_gpu`, wait in `copy_to_vram_pool_platform()` before `copy_from_textures()` via the already-proven `gpu.queue.as_hal::<Vulkan>()` → `add_wait_semaphore(...)` pattern from the Ticket-1 `62e15ce1` backport). Base for the Reco side: `1361770abd334eeb48799bd812bb4092968af72a` (keep ZC_EXP5's layout-preservation fix — it's correct and harmless even though it didn't solve this on its own; do not revert it). Acceptance gate stays narrow: does ZC_EXP4's Vulkan-source readback go from 0 nonzero bytes to real pixel data matching the CUDA readback? Only if yes, proceed to a full visual MP4 check. Diagnostic only, no production merge, no performance work. Do not investigate further alternative zero-copy theories before this — the search space is now genuinely small (correct CUDA data + blocking CUDA copy + `cuCtxSynchronize()` + correct Vulkan initial-state handling + executed layout transition + still-zero Vulkan read leaves cross-API synchronization as the only remaining credible explanation).

## OEV — Sample-baseline testing (RunPod network-volume samples) + YOLO26 volume state, handoff to YOLO26 test ticket (13 Aug 2026)

**New production infra, merged to `main`:**
- `.github/workflows/oev-runpod-sample-baseline.yml` + `runpod_sample_baseline_remote.sh` (new files, merges `c62dee5`/`5431a84`) — RunPod follow-cam test variant that reads pre-cut sample clips from `/runpod-volume/oev-samples/<sample_set_id>/<sample_id>_{left,right}_{30,60,180}s.mp4` instead of the full-source Drive path. Inputs: `datacenter` (pinned single-DC, `EU-RO-1`/`EUR-IS-1` only — no round-robin/exclusion widening, deliberately, since the sample pack only exists on those two volumes), `sample_id`, `duration_s`, `sample_set_id` (default `GX010197-seed1384188843`). Same launch/preflight/bootstrap/Reco-SHA-gate/cleanup mechanics as `oev-runpod-followcam.yml`, verbatim. **Drive fallback added** (best-effort volume copy first; if either clip file is missing on the volume, falls through to resolving+downloading from Drive `Samples/<sample_set_id>/<sample_id>/` instead of hard-failing). Output uploaded to Drive `Baseline/` folder as `followcam_<sample_id>_<duration_s>s_run<run_id>.mp4` + matching `events.jsonl`. `runpod_sample_baseline_remote.sh` differs from `runpod_followcam_remote.sh` in exactly one place: no internal random 15-20s re-trim — the full pre-staged sample clip IS the test window. Everything else (calibrate, St Margaret's field_roi injection, stitch flags, acceptance check) reused verbatim.
- **Real bug found + fixed in the frozen `runpod_bootstrap.sh`** (commit `f4b2e4f`): the FFmpeg-version-after-install check re-ran `ffmpeg -version | head -1 | grep -qE ...` a second time instead of reusing the already-captured `$FFMPEG_VER` — under `set -o pipefail`, `head -1` closing early on a long real build-config output can SIGPIPE the still-writing `ffmpeg` process, flipping a genuinely-fine version check to FATAL. Fixed by checking the cached value via a here-string (`grep -qE ... <<< "$FFMPEG_VER"`) instead of re-invoking. Verified locally against both a large-output mock (passes) and a genuinely-old version string (still correctly fails) before pushing. Same SIGPIPE-under-pipefail bug class as the one fixed 12 Aug in `runpod_gpu_preflight.sh`'s Vulkan/CUDA/NVDEC/ORT checks — this occurrence in `runpod_bootstrap.sh` had not been audited/fixed in that earlier pass.

4. **Zero-copy NV12 corruption bug — allocation-size hypothesis REJECTED with evidence; decode-side data confirmed valid; next step is the Reco #136 sync-race experiment.**
   - **Cycle 1** (`video-stitcher@470df780` on `diag/zero-copy-frame0-readback`, log-only `ZC_DIAG` added to `create_shared_texture()`; run `31792191879`): hit `vkAllocateMemory (import fd): ERROR_INVALID_EXTERNAL_HANDLE` before the new log could be checked (also suppressed by `RUST_LOG=warn`, unrelated bug). Root-caused: `calibrate.log` showed `reco_core::gpu` selected `llvmpipe (LLVM 20.1.2, 256 bits) (Vulkan)` — software Mesa renderer — while CUDA allocated from the real NVIDIA RTX 4090. `oev_zero_copy_diag_remote.sh` never ran the NVIDIA EGL ICD override that every production RunPod script (`runpod_bootstrap.sh`) runs first. Diagnostic harness gap, not a Reco bug.
   - **Cycle 2 fix** (`ffa-automations@000e1138`, harness only): reconstructs `/tmp/nvidia_egl_icd.json` + `VK_DRIVER_FILES`/`VK_ICD_FILENAMES`/`LD_LIBRARY_PATH` verbatim from `runpod_bootstrap.sh`, hard-aborts (exit 5) if calibrate still selects `llvmpipe`, and fixed `RUST_LOG` so `ZC_DIAG` is visible. `video-stitcher@470df780` unchanged.
   - **Cycle 2 result** (run `31792897210`, PASSED — NVIDIA RTX 4090 confirmed selected): `ZC_DIAG` fired for both Y (`3840x2160 R8Unorm`) and UV (`1920x1080 Rg8Unorm`) on both left/right sources — `shared_mem_alloc_size` (8388608/4194304, post CUDA VMM granularity rounding) exceeded `mem_reqs.size` (8294400/4147200) in every case: **`PASS_vs_mem_reqs=PASS` and `PASS_vs_pitch_x_height=PASS` across the board.** Allocation-size hypothesis rejected — the CUDA buffer was never undersized relative to Vulkan's requirement. `vkAllocateMemory` succeeded this run (no `ERROR_INVALID_EXTERNAL_HANDLE`), confirming that error was purely the llvmpipe harness gap, not a genuine external-handle bug.
   - **Frame0 readback evidence** (same run, `diag_dump/{left,right}_frame0_y_3840x2160.raw`): both planes show real, varied pixel data — left `min=0 max=255 mean=89.51 nonzero_bytes=8283567/8294400`, right `min=0 max=255 mean=76.65 nonzero_bytes=8292310/8294400`. Not blank, not uniform green. **CUDA decode is writing valid Y-plane data into the shared texture at frame 0** — this rules out "NVDEC/CUDA source planes already corrupt" (doc's Experiment 3 question, effectively answered early).
   - **Conclusion / next step:** allocation-size bug rejected, decode-side data confirmed valid → per the agreed decision gate, next is the originally-shelved Reco #136 experiment (`device.poll(Wait)` immediately before the zero-copy Y/UV slot is released back to the decoder, on `diag/zero-copy-frame0-readback`) to test the slot-release/sync-race hypothesis for where the corruption is actually introduced (between valid decode-side data and the final corrupted output). Not yet implemented — awaiting go, this would be cycle 3 of this chat's 3-cycle debug budget.

   **Cycle 3 (run `31793792281`, commit `101509e2` on `diag/zero-copy-frame0-readback`): #136 sync-race hypothesis REJECTED.** Added one behavioral change only -- `self.core.gpu().device().poll(wgpu::PollType::wait_indefinitely())` in `render_gpu_resident()` (`crates/reco-core/src/session/frame_processing.rs`), immediately before the left/right GPU slot-free sends, after `submit_render_output()`. Workflow updated to pull back `followcam_diag.mp4` + `events_diag.jsonl` for actual visual validation (previous cycles only had frame0 readback).
   - **Frame0 readback: still valid** (left `mean=89.51 nonzero=8283567/8294400`, right `mean=76.65 nonzero=8292310/8294400` -- identical to cycle 2, as expected since decode-side data was never in question).
   - **FPS: 9.9 fps (1122 frames in 113.0s)** vs cycle 2's 9.3 fps (1122 frames in 120.5s) -- the blocking wait cost is within run-to-run noise, not a real regression. Confirms the pipeline wasn't meaningfully bottlenecked by lack of synchronization here.
   - **Output video: still uniformly green across the entire clip.** Extracted and visually inspected frames at n=0, 300, 600, 900, 1100 (start, three mid-clip points, near-end) from `followcam_diag.mp4` (1920x1080, 1122 frames, 18.7s) -- every sampled frame is flat green (`#0b5e07`-ish, PNG size ~8.6KB each, consistent with a single solid color). The `device.poll(Wait)` fix did **not** change the output at all.
   - **Conclusion:** the Reco #136 slot-release/sync-race hypothesis is rejected -- forcing full GPU completion before slot reuse made no difference to the corruption, and cost was negligible either way. Combined with cycle 2 (allocation size confirmed adequate, frame0 CUDA decode data confirmed valid/varied, not blank or already-green), the picture is now: **valid pixel data provably exists in the shared CUDA/Vulkan texture at frame 0, and the final encoded output is provably uniform green from frame 0 onward.** The corruption is introduced somewhere between the shared texture being bound for rendering and the final NV12-converted/encoded frame -- i.e. inside the actual render pass (`render_gpu_frame_at_pose`/pipeline shader consuming the shared texture), the NV12 conversion/pack step (`nv12_converter.convert_and_readback`), or a format/binding mismatch specific to the *shared* (CUDA-imported) texture path vs. a normally-allocated texture. Not yet localized further.
   - **Next step (not started, this chat's 3-cycle debug budget is now spent):** narrow within the render/convert path specifically -- e.g. a diagnostic readback *after* the render pass consumes the shared texture (post-render, pre-NV12-convert) to bisect whether the render pass itself already produces green, or whether NV12 conversion/encode corrupts otherwise-correct rendered output. Reco issue #103 (no correct CUDA→Vulkan cross-API sync barrier beyond simple completion waiting -- `cuCtxSynchronize()` doesn't guarantee Vulkan-side visibility) is now a stronger candidate than #136, since #136's proposed fix (wait before reuse) didn't help; #103 would manifest as Vulkan seeing stale/undefined memory content *during the render itself*, not just a reuse-timing race, which a single `device.poll(Wait)` before slot release wouldn't fix. Not yet investigated.

   **Cycle 4 (run `31794753966`, commit `4f646198`): attempt 1 hit `CUDA_ERROR_NO_DEVICE` on NVDEC/NVENC (bad host, 0 frames decoded, not evidence). Attempt 2 (same commit, plain retry, fresh pod) SUCCEEDED and gave the cleanest result of this whole investigation.**
   - Added frame0 UV-plane readback/dump alongside the existing Y-plane readback (same mechanism, same trigger, upstream of the cycle-3 `device.poll(Wait)`).
   - **UV readback: `3840x1080, min=54 max=196 mean=124.63/128.30, nonzero_bytes=4147200/4147200 (100%)`, both left and right** -- real, varied, plausible chroma data, not blank/zero/clamped.
   - **Reconstructed frame 0 from the raw Y+UV dumps** (concatenated into NV12, decoded with ffmpeg `-f rawvideo -pix_fmt nv12`) for both left and right sources: **both are perfectly normal, correct images of the pitch** -- trees, sky, players, fence, full color and detail, zero corruption. Not green, not garbled.
   - **`followcam_diag.mp4` from this same run is still uniformly green** (checked frame 0/600/1100, same as cycles 2-3).
   - **Conclusion: definitive.** The CUDA decode → shared-memory copy path (Y and UV both) is 100% correct at the point it hands off to Vulkan. The corruption is conclusively introduced somewhere between "Vulkan/wgpu reading the shared texture" and "final encoded NV12 output" -- i.e. inside `render_gpu_frame_at_pose()`/the render pipeline shader that consumes `gpu_bind_groups`/the shared texture views, or inside `nv12_converter.convert_and_readback()`'s render-target → NV12 conversion. This rules out CUDA/NVDEC, rules out allocation sizing (cycle 2), and rules out the #136 slot-reuse race (cycle 3) as the root cause -- all three are now closed.
   - Secondary note: this run measured 3.4 fps (vs. 9.3-9.9 fps in cycles 2-3) -- likely just a slower/busier host on this attempt, not a code-path difference (same commit both times); not concluded, not worth chasing on its own.
   - **Next step (not started):** bisect *inside* the render/convert boundary -- e.g. an intermediate readback of the render target immediately after the render pass but before `nv12_converter` runs, to determine whether the render pass itself already produces green (shader/bind-group/format issue reading the shared texture) or whether an otherwise-correct rendered frame gets corrupted during NV12 conversion/pack. Given the shared texture formats are `R8Unorm` (Y) and `Rg8Unorm` (UV) -- confirmed correct via `ZC_DIAG` across all cycles -- a first place to check is whether the render pipeline's shader/sampler is reading the `Rg8Unorm` UV texture with the swizzle/channel order it actually expects.

   **Cycle 5 (run `31797643053`, commit `1ad40d3d`): instrumented the WRONG code path -- architectural finding, not evidence, but an important correction.** Added `ZC_EXP3`: Vulkan-side frame0 readback (`copy_texture_to_buffer`/`map_async`) of `self.gpu_shared_textures`, placed in `render_gpu_resident()`'s "shared texture path (non-buffered / immediate mode)" branch, immediately before `render_gpu_frame_at_pose()`.
   - Run completed clean (10.2 fps, 1122 frames, no crash) but **zero `ZC_EXP3` log lines appeared** -- not a bug in the diagnostic, the code path never executed.
   - **Root cause: `render_gpu_resident()` has two branches.** `if let Some(vram_idx) = self.current_vram_slot { ... return Ok(()); }` (VRAM pool path) fires first and returns early whenever the VRAM pool is active -- confirmed via `stitch.log`: `VramPool: 139 stereo NV12 slots at 3840x2160`, `Lookahead: pre-filling 90 frames`. This is the **default/always-active path** whenever `--lookahead` is set (every run in this investigation has used the default `--lookahead 1.5`). The "shared texture path" ZC_EXP3 was placed in is effectively dead code under normal operation.
   - **The real Vulkan-consumption point, found by tracing the VRAM pool path:** `VramPool::copy_from_textures()` (`crates/reco-core/src/session/vram_pool.rs`), called from `copy_to_vram_pool_platform()` (`crates/reco-core/src/session/frame_processing.rs:762`, Linux branch). This does a `copy_texture_to_texture` **from the CUDA-imported shared textures (`self.gpu_shared_textures`) into a fresh, regular (non-shared) VRAM pool texture** -- immediately followed by an *already-existing* `gpu.device.poll(wgpu::PollType::wait_indefinitely())` at frame_processing.rs:770. After this copy, all subsequent rendering reads from the ordinary pool texture, never the shared one again.
   - **Why this matters:** a readback here can distinguish two materially different bugs that cycles 1-5 could not: (a) read the **shared source texture** at this exact call site (Vulkan's real view, at the point it's actually used) -- same question as ZC_EXP3 intended, now at the correct location; (b) read the **pool destination texture** immediately after the copy+existing-wait -- if already corrupted, `copy_texture_to_texture` itself is where CUDA->Vulkan visibility breaks (strong #103 confirmation); if clean, the corruption is downstream of *all* interop, inside the ordinary (non-shared-memory) render/NV12 path -- which would rule out #103 too and point at the render pipeline/NV12 shader itself as a plain bug unrelated to zero-copy interop.
   - **Not yet implemented.** This is the immediate next step for a fresh chat: move ZC_EXP3-style readback (same staging-buffer/map_async pattern, already proven correct) into `copy_to_vram_pool_platform()`, reading both the `shared_tex[...]` sources and the pool slot destination, frame 0 only, same env-gating. No fix, diagnostic only, same as all prior cycles.

   **Cycle 6 (run `31798570666`, commits `79667aac`/`06d1417a`): DECISIVE. Vulkan's own view of the CUDA-shared source texture is all-zero, where CUDA's own view of the identical memory is always valid. Reconstructing that zero data reproduces the corruption's exact color mechanism.**
   - Removed cycle 5's dead `ZC_EXP3` (confirmed never executing -- see cycle 5). Re-implemented as `ZC_EXP4` at the corrected, actually-executed location: `copy_to_vram_pool_platform()` (Linux), reading the CUDA-shared **source** textures via Vulkan's own `copy_texture_to_buffer` immediately before `VramPool::copy_from_textures()`, then (intended) the plain VRAM-pool **destination** textures immediately after the existing copy+`poll(Wait)`.
   - **Source readback (fired cleanly, all 4 planes): `left_y`/`left_uv`/`right_y`/`right_uv` all `min=0 max=0 mean=0.00 nonzero_bytes=0/N` -- completely zero, both cameras, both planes.** This is the same shared-memory slot that, moments earlier in the identical pipeline (cycles 4/6 CUDA-side readback, same `RECO_DEBUG_DUMP_FRAME` gate, same frame), CUDA itself reads as valid, varied, real pixel data every single time. Vulkan's own copy of the exact same imported memory sees nothing.
   - **Reconstructed the zero NV12 dump exactly as in cycle 4** (`ffmpeg -f rawvideo -pix_fmt nv12`): decodes to solid `RGB(0, 136, 0)` -- pure green, the same qualitative color family as every corrupted `followcam_diag.mp4` produced in this entire investigation (Johnson independently sampled the real corrupted output at `~RGB(5, 97, 3)`). **Mechanism confirmed:** an all-zero NV12 buffer (Y=0, U=0, V=0 rather than the neutral 128/128 chroma) decodes to green under any standard YUV->RGB matrix, because zeroed chroma reads as a large negative U/V offset from center. This is not just "Vulkan sees corrupt data" -- it explains *why the corruption is specifically green* in every cycle of this investigation.
   - **Destination readback did not complete -- crashed, but on a diagnostic-only defect, not a finding.** Immediately after the 4 source reads, the process hit a fatal wgpu validation panic: `Usage flags TextureUsages(COPY_DST | TEXTURE_BINDING) of Texture with 'vram_L_Y_0' label do not contain required usage flags TextureUsages(COPY_SRC)`. The VRAM pool's slot textures (`vram_pool.rs::VramPool::new`) are created with only `COPY_DST | TEXTURE_BINDING` -- correct for their real job (copy destination + render input), but reading them back via `copy_texture_to_buffer` requires `COPY_SRC`, which was never added (out of scope for read-only instrumentation without a texture-creation change). The destination read was never reached in a way that produced data; this is a diagnostic limitation, not evidence either way about the pool copy itself.
   - **Conclusion:** given the source is already fully zero *before* `copy_from_textures()` runs, the destination read is no longer essential to localize the bug -- garbage/zero in necessarily means zero out via a straight `copy_texture_to_texture`. The finding stands on the source result alone: **the CUDA write is invisible to Vulkan's own read of the same imported external memory.** This is Reco #103 (no correct CUDA->Vulkan cross-API synchronization/visibility barrier -- `cuCtxSynchronize()` guarantees CUDA-side completion only, not Vulkan-side visibility of that write through the imported `VkDeviceMemory`) as the confirmed root cause, not #136 (already rejected, cycle 3) and not an allocation-size or decode issue (rejected, cycles 2/4).
   - **Not yet attempted:** an actual fix. Per every cycle's scope so far, no fix has been implemented -- this ticket has been diagnostic-only throughout. The next chat's task is to design and test a real synchronization fix (e.g. an explicit exported/imported Vulkan semaphore signalled by CUDA after the `cuMemcpy2D`+`cuCtxSynchronize`, waited on by Vulkan before any read of the shared image -- true cross-API GPU synchronization, not the CPU-side `cuCtxSynchronize()` already in place) rather than another read-only diagnostic. `--no-zero-copy` remains the correct production setting until a fix is verified end-to-end (allocation-size PASS + Vulkan-side readback matches CUDA-side readback + visual video check, not just a green CI run).
5. `sample_02` (all 3 durations) not started — still needed to complete the original 6-run comparison matrix Johnson asked for.

## OEV Test Runtime v1 — Benchmark Pack Prep CLOSED green; Benchmark run IN FLIGHT; tiered minDownloadMbps + stale-comment fix merged, not yet dispatched (13 Aug 2026)

**Secrets-in-layers work is fully closed (confirmed this session, not reopened):** merge `4e7a9140e9894822b3d8fc81c4272831400b0b22`, standalone scan run `31685815975` green across all 47 layers, dir-mode Gitleaks scan merged.

**`oev-benchmark-pack-prep.yml` — CLOSED, green.** Two real bugs found and fixed via verified diffs:
1. `516b552` — runner never installed `ffmpeg`/`ffprobe` (only `aria2` for the download step); extract/verify steps hit `command not found`. Fixed by adding `ffmpeg` to the existing apt-get line.
2. `32c272a` — equivalence check compared `ffprobe -show_entries format=duration` (container duration, includes AAC audio-tail overhang past the video's `-c copy` cut) against a reference number that was actually read off ffmpeg's own encode-progress line (video-stream duration), not ffprobe. Different metrics, always would have mismatched. Fixed by switching the check to `-select_streams v:0 -show_entries stream=duration` so it measures the same thing the reference value represents. Root-caused by pulling `segment.log` from the actual reference run (`31608010277`'s artifact) and confirming its `18.99s` came from ffmpeg's `time=` line, not ffprobe.
- Confirmed green: run `31691178053`, all 8 steps passed, benchmark pack uploaded to `Benchmarks/oev-test-runtime-v1/` in Drive.
- **3 fix/redispatch-cycle budget for the "close the benchmark" ticket was spent across these two fixes plus the network-timeout fix below (3 total). Do not treat pack-prep as needing further cycles — it's done.**

**`oev-test-runtime-benchmark.yml` — IN PROGRESS, real infra finding + two improvements merged, not yet redispatched:**
- **Image size confirmed via GHCR manifest query:** `ghcr.io/jhnsono/oev-test-runtime:v1-reco-53fe10f5` is 47 layers, `19,662,285,233` bytes (~19.7GB) compressed. This is why pulls are slow — not a broken pipeline, a genuinely large image.
- Run `31692246338` (before the network-timeout fix): failed — all 3 internal pod-allocation attempts hit `wait_for_network`'s old `300s` (5min) ceiling while still mid-image-pull. Root-caused via job log (`pod ... never reported a public IP + SSH mapping within 300s`) cross-referenced against user-supplied RunPod console screenshots showing `"still fetching image"` for the entire window — confirmed same event, not a guess.
- **Fix `a94d651`:** raised `wait_for_network`'s default `timeout_s` 300→1200 and job `timeout-minutes` 60→90 to give the large pull a real chance to complete and to actually measure download time (a stated deliverable) rather than truncating it.
- Redispatched as run `31694920927` (started `2026-08-13T11:18:32Z`) using the fixed timeout. **Still in progress as of this entry.** User-supplied logs show attempt 1 timed out at ~1200s as expected, attempt 2 began ~`11:38:51Z`, also slow-pulling the same image — two consecutive hosts both slow on the same 19.7GB pull, not a one-host fluke.
- **Fix `9cebd1e` (this session, NOT YET dispatched — merged to main only, current run 31694920927 unaffected since GH Actions already snapshotted the script at dispatch time):**
  1. **Tiered `minDownloadMbps` fallback**, mirroring the same start-strict-then-relax pattern the GPU fallback list uses: attempt 1 requires ≥800Mbps, attempt 2 ≥400Mbps, attempt 3 no floor (take whatever's available rather than striking out entirely). Confirmed via RunPod's official REST v1 docs that `minDownloadMbps` is a real, supported `POST /pods` field. Implemented as `MIN_DOWNLOAD_MBPS_BY_ATTEMPT = [800, 400, None]` indexed by attempt number, passed into `create_pod()`.
  2. **Corrected a stale/false code comment.** The workflow claimed the multi-GPU-fallback-list + `gpuTypePriority:'custom'` request shape was a "confirmed NVDEC-breaking" pattern, and used that as the reason this workflow only requests a single RTX 4090. That theory was falsified by run `31590998085` (same single-type request, NVDEC still failed) — the real, later-confirmed pattern is **per-host/per-driver**: certain RunPod hosts on driver `570.158.01` have NVDEC broken at the hardware/driver level regardless of request shape. `oev-runpod-followcam.yml` already restored its 4-GPU fallback list on this corrected understanding; `oev-test-runtime-benchmark.yml` never did. Comment corrected to reflect this; **the single-GPU behavior itself was deliberately left unchanged** (restoring the fallback list here is flagged as a legitimate separate future improvement, not bundled into this fix).
